# Windows Toolkit scripts: run order and rollback

One manifest (`..\toolkit.json`) drives every script. Run them as files, one at a time, in a **normal** (not elevated) shell unless a row says otherwise. Quote the full path. All scripts are ASCII only, safe to run twice, take `-DryRun` (except `11`, which is dry by default and needs `-Apply`), log to `D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs\`, and write a result JSON to `..\results\<host>\`.

Command template (Windows PowerShell 5.1 or PowerShell 7):

```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "<full path to script>" [switches]
```

## Run order (Dell, then Acer)
| Step | Script | Shell | Does | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `00-preflight.ps1` | normal | read-only: machine, vault, manifest, scripts, config secret scan, plan | no changes |
| 2 | `10-install-toolkit.ps1` | normal | installs and adopts tools, the font, PSFzf, standalone uv, uv Python | UAC prompts for Git, GitHub CLI, Starship, Node. Close editors first (Git upgrade) |
| 3 | `11-node-lts-swap.ps1 -Apply` | normal, **fresh window, Claude Code and editors closed** | Dell only: Node 22 to LTS 24 | by hand; two UAC prompts; npm globals survive |
| 4 | `20-apply-configs.ps1` | normal | profiles (managed block), Starship, git ignore, delta include, bat, fd, Terminal merge, git settings, env vars | backups to `D:\Backup\Toolkit\<timestamp>\` |
| 5 | `99-verify.ps1 -WriteLock` | normal, fresh window | manifest versus reality; Dell writes `toolkit.lock.json` | PENDING items wait for step 6 |
| 6 | `04-admin-remove.ps1 -IncludePython -Apply` | **elevated** | Dell only: retires Python 3.13.5 and Chocolatey | lives in `D:\WSL\ops\windows`; refuses until a standalone uv exists |
| 7 | `99-verify.ps1` again | normal, fresh window | PENDING items turn into PASS | |
| 8 | `40-build-catalog.ps1` | normal | writes `..\catalog\Tool Catalog - <host>.md` and `.json` | Phase 5 |

Only step 6 needs an elevated shell.

## What each script never does
- `10` never installs Tailscale, never touches Node 22 (step 3 does), never removes anything.
- `20` never deletes: a replaced file is copied to `D:\Backup\Toolkit\<timestamp>\` first. It never touches VS Code, MCP or Claude Code settings, identity or `safe.directory` entries in git, or the Build 3 cache variables.
- `40` never prints `.bashrc`, environment values of secrets, MCP arguments or URLs, and never shuts WSL down.

## Placement (Build 3 layout contract)
Every tool here is small, installer-forced to C: (MSIX, MSI, per-user portable) or already placed by Build 3. Per-user fonts (27 TTFs, about 380 MB) cannot be redirected. Nothing in this toolkit creates a junction. Caches stay on D: through the Build 3 variables, which the scripts verify but never change.

## Deviations from the earlier design notes (live check won)
- The Starship and zoxide guards use a PowerShell global variable, not `$env:STARSHIP_SHELL`. Starship's init sets that environment variable, and child processes inherit it, so a guard on it would leave a nested `pwsh` inside `pwsh` without a prompt.
- The uv standalone installer is the official `uv-installer.ps1` for 0.12.19, downloaded and checked against a pinned SHA-256, run with `UV_NO_MODIFY_PATH=1`; the script adds `~\.local\bin` to the user PATH itself.
- Corepack is not enabled (needs admin under Program Files); pnpm comes from winget.
- `bat` and `fd` configs are reconstructions from the Installations note until the Acer paste-back replaces them. On the Acer an existing file is kept and reported as DIFF.

## Rollback
- Files: `powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "...\20-apply-configs.ps1" -RestoreFrom "D:\Backup\Toolkit\<timestamp>"` copies every backed-up file back.
- Git: `git config --global --unset <key>` for each key in `toolkit.json` `gitSettings`; remove the `include.path` line and the `credential.https://github.com.helper` entries with `git config --global --edit`.
- Environment variables: remove `STARSHIP_CONFIG` and `TOOLKIT_VAULT_ROOT` in System Properties, Environment Variables (user).
- Tools: `winget uninstall --id <Id> --exact`. The per-user font files are in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Fonts` (names start `IosevkaTermNerdFontMono-`) with matching values under `HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion\Fonts`.
- Node: `winget install --id OpenJS.NodeJS.22 --exact` brings Node 22 back.

## Test overrides (for maintenance only)
`20-apply-configs.ps1` accepts `-TerminalSettingsPath`, `-TestProfileDir` and `-BackupBase` so the Terminal merge and the profile block can be exercised on copies. `-Only` takes config ids (`starship,git-ignore,git-delta,bat,fd-ignore,profile-ps5,profile-pwsh,terminal,git-settings,env`) or tool ids for `10`.

- ## Left for Build 5 and later
+ ## Resumed run, 2026-10-13: Phase 1 verification, decisions, Phase 3 build

### Decisions (answered 2026-10-13: "implement every D")
D1 to D6, D11 and D13 to D16 are implemented as recommended; D7 to D10 moved to Build 5 (E1 to E4); D12 stands. Extra scope the user approved: PSFzf as a manifest module (D13), bat and fd configs (D14), and one shared `_lib.ps1` plus `11-node-lts-swap.ps1` (the Node swap is a replacement, so it is a gated script of its own).

### Live changes since the 2026-10-11/12 audit
| Item | Audit | Live 2026-10-13 |
|---|---|---|
| C: / D: free | 39.5 / 363.9 GiB | 145.4 / 450.2 GiB (Build 3 done) |
| Cache variables | unset | all set (D:\Cache\...); npm cache is `D:\Cache\npm`, not `D:\npm-cache` |
| Windows Ollama | 0.24.0 | 0.40.3 at D:\Apps\Ollama; port 11434 still answered by the WSL 0.18.3 |
| Tailscale, PowerShell 7, Starship, font, zoxide, fzf, fd, bat, delta, NanaZip, pnpm | absent | still absent (as designed) |
| Dell Command Update | not checked | 5.7.0 installed |
| `C:\Users\Anant Gupta\AGENTS.md` | not checked | does not exist (pointer line target for Phase 5) |
| WSL configs vs Acer snapshot | 13 of 17 differ | catalog probe: 29 comparable files, 10 match, 12 differ, 7 missing |

### Contradictions logged (live machine won)
1. The Acer snapshot (10-11) is stale. Vault notes written 2026-10-12 say the Acer already has PowerShell 7 and 5.1 profiles (Starship, zoxide, UTF-8, VS Code hook), fzf, fd, bat, delta, zoxide, PSFzf, `%APPDATA%\bat\config`, `%APPDATA%\fd\ignore`, delta in its Windows `.gitconfig`, standalone uv 0.12.19 and VS Code on `D:\Apps`. The Acer run therefore starts with a read-only paste-back and keeps its own bat and fd files.
2. `VERSIONS.md` and `MANIFEST.md` exist in the snapshot (the 10-11 audit said they did not).
3. `04-admin-remove.ps1 -IncludePython` deletes `C:\Python313`, so the separate `pip uninstall uv` step in D2 is dropped.
4. winget portables may install without a PATH entry (Acer, 2026-10-12): `10` repairs it with a hard link in `WinGet\Links`.
5. The design guard `$env:STARSHIP_SHELL` would suppress the prompt in nested shells (the variable is inherited by child processes); the block uses a global PowerShell variable.
6. Font archive extracts to 27 TTFs, 379 MB (not the 60 MB estimate).
7. The Dell's Codex config carries a WSL path (`/mnt/c/.../node_repl`) that does not resolve on Windows; reported by `99-verify`, owned by Build 6.
8. `Md` is a built-in alias of `mkdir` in PowerShell, so a helper function with that name silently called `New-Item`.

### Files written (vault: `Old Laptop Rebuild\Windows Toolkit\`)
`toolkit.json` (47 entries), `_scripts\` (`_lib.ps1`, `00-preflight.ps1`, `10-install-toolkit.ps1`, `11-node-lts-swap.ps1`, `20-apply-configs.ps1`, `40-build-catalog.ps1`, `99-verify.ps1`, `README.md`), `configs\` (`starship.toml` and `git\ignore` byte-identical to the Acer's, SHA-256 `9EF71378...` and `546B86DB...`; `git\delta.gitconfig`; `powershell\managed-block.ps1`; `terminal\toolkit-fragment.json`; `bat\config`; `fd\ignore`), empty `results\` and `catalog\`.

### Checks run in Phase 3 (all read-only or on copies)
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Parse every script with `Parser::ParseFile`; ASCII-only byte scan | 7 of 7 clean, 0 non-ASCII bytes |
| `00-preflight.ps1` (real run) | exit 0; 16 install/upgrade actions planned, 2 PENDING (Python, Chocolatey), 1 MANUAL (Node 22) |
| `10-install-toolkit.ps1 -DryRun`, `20-apply-configs.ps1 -DryRun`, `11-node-lts-swap.ps1`, `40-build-catalog.ps1 -DryRun` | all exit 0 |
| Terminal merge on a copy of the Dell settings and on the Acer snapshot settings | scheme, font, defaults, keybindings, pwsh profile, Ubuntu rename all applied; Acer conda profiles kept; second run UNCHANGED |
| Profile block on the Dell profile copy | opam, Kiro and `New-PyEnv` kept; second run UNCHANGED |
| Profile block on an Acer-style profile (BOM, CRLF, duplicate Starship/zoxide/PSFzf/VS Code hook) | duplicates commented with `# [toolkit-superseded]`, BOM and CRLF kept, result parses clean |
| Profile with two statements on one line | MANUAL, file untouched |
| Managed block executed in clean Windows PowerShell 5.1 | loads, UTF-8 set, no error |
| uv 0.12.19 installer | downloaded to scratch, SHA-256 `F44CF877...` pinned, `UV_NO_MODIFY_PATH` supported |
| Font archive | SHA-256 matches the published `SHA-256.txt`; bsdtar wildcard extracts exactly 27 Mono TTFs |
| `99-verify.ps1` before any install | runs end to end; 47 expected FAILs (nothing installed yet), 24 MCP entries read by name only |

### Errors found while building, with root cause
| Error | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `Resolve-Cmd` returned `C` for every tool | a one-element array returned from a function is unrolled to a string, then `[0]` took its first character | wrap in `@()` and cast |
| "Argument types do not match" writing result JSON | `@(<List[object]>)` inside an `[ordered]` literal | use `.ToArray()` |
| winget version always empty for `--id` queries | with `--id`, winget prints a single-space table, so splitting on two spaces found nothing | regex `Id\s+Version` |
| Backup file name too long | flattened full path in the leaf name | 8-char hash plus leaf name |
| `-Only a,b` matched nothing | `powershell -File` passes the comma list as one string | split on commas |
| `Md` helper created directories | alias precedence over functions | renamed `MdCell` |
| WSL probe returned no hashes | a probed tool read stdin, which was the piped script itself | `</dev/null` on each probe |
| `Contains` overload error reading MCP JSON | `Dictionary` has `ContainsKey`, not `Contains` | test `Keys -contains` |
| Stderr "#< CLIXML" counted as a profile error | PowerShell wraps progress in CLIXML on redirected stderr | strip it, keep real error text |

## Left for Build 5 and later

```, all the scripts were run. The first script was run perfectly and the rest are provided to you over here. The elevated shell along with the normal shell outputs. The terminal settings have been configured are seen as shown correctly. Now, let's make sure that all the scripts are run perfectly. Making sure that this phase has been completed. Go through the outputs provided in detail but also look through the information yourself and provide me with the last set of scripts that need to run to close this phase off.

## Claude

Reading the actual logs and result files first, not just the pasted snippets.

Two causes found: Node's MSI refused with "Error 1730: you must be an Administrator", so the swap has to run from the elevated shell, and `uv-python` was a false positive. Now the 99-verify results.

Now the `git-pager` check in 99 and the elevation requirement in 11.

Now the PATH-order step in `20-apply-configs.ps1` (manifest entry, function, and a matching check in 99).

Now the two defects in `04-admin-remove.ps1`: the uv gate that can never pass, and the Machine PATH rewrite that would flatten `%SystemRoot%` entries. I'm backing the script up first.

Parse-checking everything that changed, then dry runs of the new and changed steps.

Recording the Phase 4 review in the findings note.

## Phase 4 is not closed yet: three checks still fail, and the cause of each is understood

Everything in the Dell install and config runs worked. The three failures are a Node uninstall that needs an elevated shell, a missing lock file that depends on it, and a wrong check in `99-verify`. Reading the logs also turned up two more problems that your run did not hit yet. All of them are fixed and dry-run tested, but only your runs will prove the fixes.

### What the runs showed
**Worked:**
- Everything in `10-install-toolkit` installed, except Node, which is a hand step.
- All config files hash-match the Acer's.
- Both PowerShell profiles load with one Starship and zoxide init.
- The Terminal merge is correct, as you confirmed.

**Failed, with causes:**

| Failure | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Node 22 uninstall (exit 1603) | The event log shows MSI error 1730, "You must be an Administrator to remove this application". The MSI is per-machine and refuses a non-elevated removal. | `11 -Apply` now requires the elevated shell and refuses early otherwise. |
| `git-pager` check | `git config --global --get` ignores included files, and the pager lives in the delta include. Delta is active (`git config --get core.pager` returns `delta`). | The check now uses `--includes`. |
| `04-admin-remove` dry run: "STOP: no standalone uv" | `Get-Command uv` returns only the first match, which is the pip uv that script exists to remove, so its gate could never pass. | I patched that gate in your Build 3 script (backup in `D:\WSL\ops\windows\archive\`). |
| `lock-write` | A consequence of the Node and pager failures. | None needed. |

**Two more problems the review caught:**
- **`uv-python` false positive:** it reported "present 3.13.5", but that is the Chocolatey `C:\Python313`. uv's own folder only holds 3.11.15. Removing Python now would have left you with no 3.13. Detection now counts only uv-managed Pythons, and the patched `04` refuses to run until one exists.
- **Wrong uv would win:** Hermes' `uv` 0.11.28 is entry 2 on your user PATH, and the standalone uv is entry 11. After Python goes, `uv` would resolve to Hermes' copy. `20-apply-configs` now moves `~\.local\bin` to the front of the user PATH and saves the old value first.

**Also patched in `04`:** it rewrote the Machine PATH in a way that would have turned your `%SystemRoot%` entries into plain text. It now edits the raw registry value, keeps the type, and saves the old value to `D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs\`.

The font difference you described is recorded: the Dell uses the Mono build and the Acer the normal one. I made no change for it.

### Last set of commands
Each path is the full one; the scripts folder is `...\Windows Toolkit\_scripts\`.

- # ------------------------------------------------------------------ run
try {
+ function Apply-PathOrder {
    # Puts the preferred folder (the standalone uv in ~\.local\bin) first on the USER path, keeping the registry value type
    # and every other entry. The old value is saved to the backup folder first. Machine PATH is not touched here.
    param($Cfg)
    $want = [Environment]::ExpandEnvironmentVariables($Cfg.prefer)
    $key = [Microsoft.Win32.Registry]::CurrentUser.OpenSubKey('Environment', $true)
    try {
        $raw = [string]$key.GetValue('Path', '', [Microsoft.Win32.RegistryValueOptions]::DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames)
        $new = Get-PathWithFirst -RawPath $raw -Preferred $want
        if ($new -ceq $raw) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'UNCHANGED' ("{0} is already first on the user PATH" -f $want); return }
        if ($dry) { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'PLAN' ("[DRYRUN] would move {0} to the front of the user PATH (all other entries kept in order)" -f $want); return }
        $base = $global:TK.BackupBase; if (-not $base) { $base = 'D:\Backup\Toolkit' }
        $dir = Join-Path $base $global:TK.Ts
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $dir | Out-Null
        $bk = Join-Path $dir 'user-path-before.txt'
        Write-Utf8NoBom -Path $bk -Text $raw
        $kind = $key.GetValueKind('Path')
        $key.SetValue('Path', $new, $kind)
        [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('TOOLKIT_PATH_REFRESH', '1', 'User')
        [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('TOOLKIT_PATH_REFRESH', $null, 'User')
        Update-SessionPath
        $chk = [string]$key.GetValue('Path', '', [Microsoft.Win32.RegistryValueOptions]::DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames)
        $first = ($chk -split ';' | Where-Object { $_ } | Select-Object -First 1)
        if ([Environment]::ExpandEnvironmentVariables($first).TrimEnd('\') -ieq $want.TrimEnd('\')) {
            Add-Result $Cfg.id 'CHANGED' ("{0} is now first on the user PATH; old value saved to {1}; open a new window to use it" -f $want, $bk) @{ backup = $bk }
        } else { Add-Result $Cfg.id 'FAIL' ("PATH did not re-read as expected; old value is in {0}" -f $bk) }
    } finally { $key.Close() }
}

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ run
try {

- # ---------------------------------------------------------------- MCP inventory (names and command names only)
+ # ---------------------------------------------------------------- PATH helper
function Get-PathWithFirst {
    # Pure string function. Returns RawPath with the Preferred folder first; other entries keep their order and text
    # (so %VARIABLES% stay unexpanded) and later duplicates of the preferred folder are dropped.
    param([string]$RawPath, [string]$Preferred)
    $want = $Preferred.TrimEnd('\')
    $entries = @($RawPath -split ';' | Where-Object { $_ })
    $head = $Preferred
    $rest = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.List[string]
    $taken = $false
    foreach ($e in $entries) {
        if ([Environment]::ExpandEnvironmentVariables($e).TrimEnd('\') -ieq $want) {
            if (-not $taken) { $head = $e; $taken = $true }
            continue
        }
        $rest.Add($e)
    }
    return ((@($head) + $rest.ToArray()) -join ';')
}

```diff
- ## Left for Build 5 and later
+ ## Phase 4: Dell run review (2026-10-13, 00:52 to 01:06)

### State-changing commands the user ran, with results
| Time | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 00:52 | `10-install-toolkit.ps1` | exit 0. Installed: PowerShell 7.6.6, Starship 1.26.0, IosevkaTerm NFM (27 TTFs, 379.2 MB, SHA-256 verified), zoxide 0.10.0, fzf 0.74.4, PSFzf 2.7.12 (5.1 and 7), fd 10.5.0, bat 0.26.1, delta 0.20.1, NanaZip 7.0.1843, pnpm 12.11.2, standalone uv 0.12.19. Upgraded: Git 2.52.0 to 2.55.0, GitHub CLI 2.89.0 to 2.102.0. Node left MANUAL |
| 01:00 | `11-node-lts-swap.ps1 -Apply` (twice) | first stopped on 12 running `node.exe` (children of `cmd`, started by MCP servers); second reached the uninstall and failed, see errors |
| 01:02 | `20-apply-configs.ps1` | exit 0. 11 CHANGED: starship.toml, git ignore, delta include, bat, fd, both profiles (backup of the Dell profile in `D:\Backup\Toolkit\20261013-010211\`), Terminal merge, 10 git settings, `STARSHIP_CONFIG`, `TOOLKIT_VAULT_ROOT`. Build 3 cache variables untouched and correct |
| 01:04 | `99-verify.ps1 -WriteLock` | 97 PASS, 3 FAIL, 3 PENDING, 1 WARN; lock not written |
| 01:06 | `04-admin-remove.ps1 -IncludePython` (elevated, dry run) | printed `STOP: no standalone uv found yet` |

Confirmed good by the run: all hashes match the Acer's (starship `9EF71378...`, ignore `546B86DB...`), both profiles load with one Starship init (5.1 in 2.5 s because of opam, 7 in 1.3 s), `starship prompt` renders, font family registered in a fresh process, Terminal shows the new scheme and font (user confirmed visually).

### Errors found in the run, with root cause
| Error | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Node 22 uninstall: winget exit 1603 | the event log says MSI error 1730, "You must be an Administrator to remove this application"; the MSI was installed per machine and the non-elevated removal is refused | `11 -Apply` now requires an elevated shell and refuses early otherwise |
| `uv-python` reported "present 3.13.5" | `uv python list` also lists system interpreters; 3.13.5 is the Chocolatey `C:\Python313`. uv's own folder holds only 3.11.15, so removing Python would have left no 3.13 | detection now counts only folders in `uv python dir`; `10 -Only uv-python` installs 3.13 |
| `git-pager` FAIL | `git config --global --get` does not follow includes; the pager is set in `delta.gitconfig`. Delta is active (`git config --get core.pager` returns `delta`) | check uses `--includes` |
| 04 dry run: "no standalone uv found" | `Get-Command uv` returns only the first match, the pip copy being removed, so the gate could never pass | script patched (backup `D:\WSL\ops\windows\archive\04-admin-remove.ps1.bak-20261013`): `Get-Command -All` plus the standalone folder, and a uv-managed Python 3.13 is now required |
| (found by review) Machine PATH rewrite | `04` wrote the expanded PATH back with `SetEnvironmentVariable`, which turns a REG_EXPAND_SZ value with `%SystemRoot%` entries into plain text | rewrites the raw registry value as ExpandString and saves the old value |
| (found by review) wrong uv would win after Python is gone | Hermes' uv 0.11.28 is entry 2 of the user PATH, the standalone uv entry 11 | new `path-order` step in `20` moves `~\.local\bin` to the front of the user PATH, old value saved |
| Syncthing conflict copy of a result JSON | two quick writes of the same `latest` file; byte-identical | removed |

### Machine difference noted by the user
Terminal font face: the Dell uses `IosevkaTerm NFM` (Mono build); the Acer uses `IosevkaTerm Nerd Font` (normal build). Recorded as a DIFF with this reason for the parity matrix; no script change was made for it. Open for Phase 6: whether the Acer run keeps its own face.

## Left for Build 5 and later
```

## Claude

Understood: the Dell's Terminal face becomes exactly `IosevkaTerm Nerd Font`, as on the Acer. That family is in the same Nerd Fonts archive we already verified, as the `IosevkaTermNerdFont-*.ttf` files (the non-Mono set). Before I wire it in, I'll read the font's own name table so the script checks for the name Windows actually registers.

My own edit put unescaped quotes into a JSON string. Fixing it and re-validating.

The Dell's Terminal face will now be `IosevkaTerm Nerd Font`, the same as the Acer. I changed the scripts and ran the dry runs, but the font itself is not installed yet. Run the three commands below to do that.

## Why Terminal asked you to install it

The Dell only has the Mono set (`IosevkaTerm NFM`), and the Acer's name belongs to the normal set. I read the font files directly to confirm:

| Set | Windows family name | Name Terminal matches |
|---|---|---|
| Normal (`IosevkaTermNerdFont-*.ttf`) | `IosevkaTerm NF` | **`IosevkaTerm Nerd Font`** |
| Mono (`IosevkaTermNerdFontMono-*.ttf`) | `IosevkaTerm NFM` | `IosevkaTerm Nerd Font Mono` |

## There is no installer folder, so use the command

The fonts only exist inside the Nerd Fonts v3.5.1 download we already hash-verified. The installer script now takes the normal set from that same archive, and the Mono set stays installed because VS Code uses it. It skips files that are already present, since Terminal holds the Mono files open.

Cost: another 27 files, about 380 MB on C:.

## Claude

First the `wsl ~` failure, since it's blocking you. Reproducing it in both shells before I change anything.

The wrapper handles every case. Adding it to the managed profile block (source in the vault; you re-apply it with one command).

I now have the full picture. Applying the corrections: first the Build 5 prompt (stale facts and the gaps the audit found), then the Index, the Build 2 notes and Installations.

Build 5 is ready to send, and the `wsl ~` failure is fixed in the scripts. You need to run one command to apply it.

## The `wsl ~` failure

- **Cause:** PowerShell 7, your new default shell, turns a bare `~` into `C:\Users\Anant Gupta` before `wsl.exe` sees it. WSL then tries to run that path as a command.
- **Why it used to work:** Windows PowerShell 5.1 doesn't do this expansion.
- **Fix:** I added a small `wsl` wrapper to the managed profile block. It turns a leading `~` into `--cd '~'` and passes everything else through. I tested `wsl ~`, `wsl -e`, `wsl -d Ubuntu --`, `wsl --cd /tmp` and `wsl -l -q` through it, and they all work.
- **To apply it** (normal shell), then open a new window:

```powershell
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Old Laptop Rebuild\Windows Toolkit\_scripts\20-apply-configs.ps1" -Only profile-ps5,profile-pwsh
```

- **Until then:** `wsl --cd '~'` works in any shell.

## What the audit found

I read every note under `Old Laptop Rebuild` and `New Laptop` and compared each claim with the live machine. Builds 1 to 3 are complete apart from the open items below.

**Stale facts, now corrected:**
- **WSL version:** the live WSL is **3.0.3.0 stable**, with systemd `running` and zero failed units. The Build 2 gate and the "wait for a stable release" decision are closed.
- **`core.autocrlf` in WSL:** already `input` since 2026-10-07, so Build 5's E5 only verifies it.
- **`core.excludesfile`:** Build 2 said it was set, but it's unset. Git reads the default `~/.config/git/ignore`, which has the same eight patterns, so it's harmless.
- **Build 5 script source:** the Index said Build 5's parity script comes from Build 4. Build 4 no longer carries any WSL script, so Build 5 writes its own.

**Genuinely missed or open:**
- **Unapplied corrections:** the six "Corrections older notes need" in the Build 2 findings were never applied. They are now on the Index stale list for Build 5's closing phase.
- **`ConversationCapture-Backfill-WSL`:** this Windows task still runs every 30 minutes. Build 1 flagged it and Build 3 never decided. It's now a report-only item for you in Build 5.
- **Windows startup apps:** Discord, Spotify, Rize, Voquill, Slack and Docker Desktop still start with Windows, though Build 3 recommended disabling them.
- **Docker Desktop auto-start:** [[VS Code - Windows]] says it's off by choice, but the Run key starts it.
- **Malwarebytes:** version 5.7 is still installed.
- **.NET 8:** removal still waits for your Dell Command Update check.
- **Claimed done, now confirmed:** the Copilot, Bing, telemetry and Edge policies are set, and both quarantine folders are intact. Defender exclusions and the BIOS battery cap can't be checked without admin or BIOS access.

- if ($env:TERM_PROGRAM -eq 'vscode') {
+ # PowerShell 7 turns a bare ~ into the Windows home before wsl.exe sees it ("wsl ~" then fails). Pass it through as a literal.
function wsl {
    if ($args.Count -gt 0 -and ($args[0] -ceq '~' -or $args[0] -ieq $HOME)) {
        $toolkitRest = @($args | Select-Object -Skip 1)
        & "$env:SystemRoot\System32\wsl.exe" --cd '~' @toolkitRest
    } else { & "$env:SystemRoot\System32\wsl.exe" @args }
}
if ($env:TERM_PROGRAM -eq 'vscode') {

du -sh ~/.vscode-server ~/.cursor-server ~/.vscode-remote-containers ~/.npm ~/.npm/_npx ~/projects 2\u003e/dev/null\necho \"== ~ stubs\"; wc -c ~/AGENTS.md 2\u003e/dev/null; ls ~/tools 2\u003e/dev/null\necho \"== config hashes vs Acer\"; do [ -f ~/$f ] \u0026\u0026 echo \"have $f\" || echo \"MISSING $f\"; done\necho \"== bashrc marker lines (marker names only)\"; grep -n \[REDACTED]\u0027 ~/.bashrc | cut -c1-110\necho \"== repos\"; find ~/projects -name .git -maxdepth 6 2\u003e/dev/null | wc -l\necho \"== wsl.conf\"; bash /tmp/tkprobe.sh \u003c/dev/null; rm -f /tmp/tkprobe.sh\" 2\u003e\u00261\n($o -join \"`n\") -replace \"`0\",\u0027\u0027","description":"Read-only probe of the live WSL state against the notes","timeout":240000}`
- `PowerShell` -- `{"command":"\"--- D:\\WSL\\ops\"; Get-ChildItem D:\\WSL\\ops -Force | % { \"{0}  {1,10}  {2}\" -f $_.LastWriteTime.ToString(\u0027MM-dd HH:mm\u0027),$_.Length,$_.Name }\n\"--- .wslconfig\"; Get-Content \"$env:USERPROFILE\\.wslconfig\"\n\"--- WSL version\"; (wsl.exe --version) -replace \"`0\",\u0027\u0027 | Select-Object -First 4\n\"--- tarball\"; Get-Item D:\\WSL\\Installers\\wsl-ubuntu.tar -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | % { \"{0} bytes {1}\" -f $_.Length,$_.LastWriteTime }\n\"--- ext4.vhdx / swap\"; Get-Item D:\\WSL\\Ubuntu\\ext4.vhdx,D:\\WSL\\swap.vhdx | % { \"{0}  {1:N2} GiB\" -f $_.Name,($_.Length/1GB) }\n\"--- scheduled tasks (Conversation/Jarvis/Toolkit)\"; Get-ScheduledTask | ? { $_.TaskName -match \u0027Conversation|Jarvis|Toolkit|Syncthing|ClaudeKit|SecondBrain\u0027 } | % { \"{0,-40} {1}\" -f $_.TaskName,$_.State }\n\"--- D roots\"; Get-ChildItem D:\\ -Force -Directory | % Name\n\"--- Google Drive running?\"; @(Get-Process GoogleDriveFS -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue).Count","description":"Read-only Windows-side facts that Build 5 depends on"}`
- `PowerShell` -- `{"command":"$sh=@\u0027\necho \"== systemd detail\"; systemctl is-system-running; systemctl --failed --no-legend | wc -l; systemctl status systemd-binfmt.service --no-pager 2\u003e/dev/null | sed -n \u00271,4p\u0027 | cut -c1-110\necho \"== crontab (command column only, no env)\"; crontab -l 2\u003e/dev/null | grep -v \u0027^#\u0027 | sed -E \u0027s/^([^ ]+ +){5}//\u0027 | cut -c1-100\necho \"== user timers\"; systemctl --user list-timers --no-legend 2\u003e/dev/null | cut -c1-120\necho \"== wslview\"; wslview --version 2\u003e\u00261 | head -2\necho \"== journal size\"; journalctl --disk-usage 2\u003e/dev/null | cut -c1-90\necho \"== core.excludesfile\"; git config --global --get core.excludesfile; ls -la ~/.config/git/ignore | cut -c1-90\necho \"== mcp servers (claude user scope names)\"; cd /tmp \u0026\u0026 timeout 60 claude mcp list 2\u003e/dev/null | sed -E \u0027s/(https?:\\/\\/)[^ ]+/\\1.../\u0027 | cut -c1-100 | head -14\necho \"== tunnel service\"; ~/tools/vscode-tunnel-cli/code tunnel status 2\u003e\u00261 | head -3 | cut -c1-100\necho \"== sandbox setting\"; grep -c \u0027\"sandbox\"\u0027 ~/.claude/settings.json 2\u003e/dev/null\n\u0027@\n$b64=[Convert]::ToBase64String([Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes(($sh -replace \"`r`n\",\"`n\")))\n((\u0026 \"$env:SystemRoot\\System32\\wsl.exe\" -d Ubuntu -- bash -lc \"echo $b64 | base64 -d \u003e /tmp/tkp2.sh; bash /tmp/tkp2.sh \u003c/dev/null; rm -f /tmp/tkp2.sh\" 2\u003e\u00261) -join \"`n\") -replace \"`0\",\u0027\u0027\n\"=========== build2-sudo-step.sh content\"; Get-Content D:\\WSL\\ops\\build2-sudo-step.sh\n\"=========== build1-sudo-step.sh (first 25 lines)\"; Get-Content D:\\WSL\\ops\\build1-sudo-step.sh -TotalCount 25","description":"Probe cron, timers, MCP, tunnel and read the unrun sudo scripts","timeout":240000}`

{ $_.DisplayName -match \u0027Microsoft 365|Office|OneDrive|Malwarebytes|Respondus|WinRAR|MongoDB|Dell Optimizer|SupportAssist|Edge$|Microsoft Edge|IntelliJ\u0027 } | % { \"$($_.DisplayName) $($_.DisplayVersion)\" } | Sort-Object -Unique) -join \"`n\"\n\"--- Dell Command Update / .NET 8 gate\"\n($u | ? - `PowerShell` -- `{"command":"$nl=\u0027D:\\Users\\_Anant\\10_Areas\\Documents\\Jarvis\\40_Resources\\CS\\Concepts\\New Laptop\u0027\n$u=New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false)\nfunction Rep($path,$old,$new){ $s=[IO.File]::ReadAllText($path); if(-not $s.Contains($old)){ \"NOT FOUND in $(Split-Path $path -Leaf): $($old.Substring(0,[math]::Min(80,$old.Length)))\"; return }; $i=$s.IndexOf($old); if($s.IndexOf($old,$i+1) -ge 0){ \"AMBIGUOUS in $(Split-Path $path -Leaf): $($old.Substring(0,[math]::Min(60,$old.Length)))\"; return }; [IO.File]::WriteAllText($path,$s.Replace($old,$new),$u); \"ok: \" + (Split-Path $path -Leaf) + \" :: \" + $old.Substring(0,[math]::Min(50,$old.Length)) }\n$p=\"$nl\\Old Laptop Rebuild\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 5 Prompt (WSL Parity and D Drive).md\"\n# backup first\n$bk=\"$nl\\Old Laptop Rebuild\\Runs\\_backups\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 5 Prompt (WSL Parity and D Drive).md.bak-20261013-readiness\"\nCopy-Item $p $bk -Force; \"backup: \" + (Split-Path $bk -Leaf)\nRep $p \u0027Dell WSL (live): WSL 3.0.1.0, kernel 6.18.40.1, Ubuntu 24.04.4,\u0027 \u0027Dell WSL (live, re-verified 2026-10-13): WSL 3.0.3.0 (the stable release of 2026-10-09), kernel 6.18.54.1, Ubuntu 24.04.4,\u0027\nRep $p \u0027systemd state is degraded because systemd-binfmt.service fails with a read-only flush; WSL issue 41783 is fixed in WSL 3.0.2, a prerelease. I chose to wait for the stable release: do not install the prerelease and do not apply a workaround.\u0027 \u0027systemd now reports running with zero failed units: the stable WSL 3.0.3 carries the fix for the systemd-binfmt read-only flush (WSL issue 41783), so the old degraded state, and my earlier decision to wait for a stable release, are closed. Re-check it in Phase 1; do not install any prerelease and do not apply workarounds. Build 2 gate 4.5 (the 75-second idle test) passed on 3.0.1; a repeat on 3.0.3 is optional and is a Windows host step for me.\u0027\nRep $p \u0027Tools on the Dell WSL: node 24.14.1\u0027 \u0027Windows side changed on 2026-10-13 (Build 4, not yours): PowerShell 7 is now the default Windows Terminal shell. PowerShell 7 expands a bare ~ before wsl.exe sees it, so \"wsl ~\" fails there; the Build 4 profile block now wraps wsl so \"wsl ~\" works again once I re-apply it, and \"wsl --cd \u0027\u0027~\u0027\u0027\" always works. Windows has Node 24.20, standalone uv 0.12.19 first on the PATH, Python and Chocolatey are gone, and Windows git has core.autocrlf=input. None of this changes the WSL side, but it is what powershell.exe will show you.\nTools on the Dell WSL: node 24.14.1\u0027\nRep $p \u0027E5 core.autocrlf: input on the Dell\u0027\u0027s WSL; the Acer\u0027\u0027s WSL stays false.\u0027 \u0027E5 core.autocrlf: input on the Dell\u0027\u0027s WSL (Build 2 already set it on 2026-10-07; verify only); the Acer\u0027\u0027s WSL stays false.\u0027\nRep $p \u0027- **Before you send it:** make sure\u0027 \u0027- **Starting it:** `wsl --cd \u0027\u0027~\u0027\u0027` (or `wsl ~` after the Build 4 profile block is re-applied), then `claude`. In Windows Terminal you can also open the Ubuntu profile, which starts in the WSL home.\n- **Before you send it:** make sure\u0027\nRep $p \u0027Known contradictions to resolve with live evidence, not by choosing a note:\u0027 \u0027Known contradictions to resolve with live evidence, not by choosing a note (the first two were already settled by the 2026-10-13 readiness audit; confirm them): WSL 3.0.1 degraded in the notes versus 3.0.3 running live; Build 2 says core.excludesfile was set, live has it unset and Git reads the default ~/.config/git/ignore (84 bytes, the same eight patterns), which is fine;\u0027\nRep $p \u0027List the Acer-dependent and sign-in items as WAITING USER without attempting them:\u0027 \u0027List the Acer-dependent, sign-in and Windows-side items as WAITING USER without attempting them: the Windows scheduled task ConversationCapture-Backfill-WSL (still Ready; it starts wsl.exe every 30 minutes, which Build 1 flagged for review and Build 3 left undecided; with the idle timers off it is harmless, so only report it and its effect),\u0027\nRep $p \u0027fix the stale statements listed in the Index under \"Stale notes to fix when touched\" by patching those notes by heading,\u0027 \u0027fix the stale statements listed in the Index under \"Stale notes to fix when touched\" (including the six items under \"Corrections older notes need\" in the Build 2 findings and the systemd statements, now resolved) by patching those notes by heading,\u0027\n(Select-String -Path $p -Pattern \u0027^updated:\u0027 | % Line)","description":"Patch the Build 5 prompt with verified live facts and the missing carry-overs"}`

It is research-first, cannot use sudo or restart WSL, and runs from `cd ~ && claude` in WSL with Sonnet 5.5 at high effort.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 differences:
     - PS7 expands a bare `~` for native commands, even from variables and splats; a quoted literal is exempt.
     - 5.1 quirks: no `&&` or ternary, `2>&1` on natives wraps ErrorRecords, `@(List[object])` inside `[ordered]` fails with "Argument types do not match" (use `.ToArray()`), `Get-Command x` returns only the first match (use `-All`), single-element arrays returned from functions are unrolled, `Md` is an alias of `mkdir`, and `-EncodedCommand` emits CLIXML on stderr.
   - winget behaviour:
     - `winget list --id` prints a single-space table, so parse with a regex rather than a two-space split.
     - Portable packages may lack a PATH entry; the repair is a hard link into `WinGet\Links`.
     - MSI Error 1730 shows as exit 1603 when a per-machine MSI is removed non-elevated.
   - Registry PATH handling:
     - REG_EXPAND_SZ with `%SystemRoot%` entries must be edited raw (`DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames`, `RegistryValueKind.ExpandString`).
     - A dummy environment variable set and unset forces a WM_SETTINGCHANGE broadcast.
   - Fonts:
     - Nerd Fonts v3.5.1 `IosevkaTerm.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `[REDACTED]`.
     - Base set: GDI family "IosevkaTerm NF", typographic "IosevkaTerm Nerd Font". Mono set: "IosevkaTerm NFM".
     - Per-user install = copy to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Fonts` plus HKCU registry values plus a WM_FONTCHANGE broadcast.
   - uv standalone installer 0.12.19: pinned installer SHA-256 `[REDACTED]`, run with `UV_NO_MODIFY_PATH=1`.
   - Managed profile block between markers `# >>> TOOLKIT MANAGED BLOCK (Build 4) >>>` and `# <<< TOOLKIT MANAGED BLOCK (Build 4) <<<`:
     - Superseded duplicate statements are commented via the AST.
     - BOM and EOL are preserved, and the result is parse-validated.
     - Guards use global PowerShell variables, not `$env:STARSHIP_SHELL`.
   - Windows Terminal settings merge (`ConvertFrom-Json`/`ConvertTo-Json`) from `toolkit-fragment.json`.
   - Git: `--includes` is needed to read included settings; the shared delta include; the gh credential helper.
   - Layout contract from Build 3b: C: = Windows/installs; `D:\Apps`, `D:\Cache\<tool>`, `D:\AI`, `D:\WSL`, `D:\Backup`, `D:\Quarantine`.
   - WSL: mirrored networking, `.wslconfig` (20 GB, 10 CPUs, 8 GB swap at `D:\WSL\swap.vhdx`, idle timeouts -1), WSL 3.0.3.0 stable (systemd-binfmt fix), Tailscale only inside Dell WSL, Syncthing for the vault, Settings Sync live.
   - wsl.exe prints UTF-16 for its own messages (strip NULs). Probe scripts were run by base64-encoding them and piping to `bash` with `</dev/null`, because a probed tool reading stdin swallowed the piped script.
   - Naming: Dell vault `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`; Acer `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis`.

       ```powershell
       # PowerShell 7 turns a bare ~ into the Windows home before wsl.exe sees it ("wsl ~" then fails). Pass it through as a literal.
       function wsl {
           if ($args.Count -gt 0 -and ($args[0] -ceq '~' -or $args[0] -ieq $HOME)) {
               $toolkitRest = @($args | Select-Object -Skip 1)
               & "$env:SystemRoot\System32\wsl.exe" --cd '~' @toolkitRest
           } else { & "$env:SystemRoot\System32\wsl.exe" @args }
       }
       ```

4. Errors and fixes:
   - `Resolve-Cmd` returned the first character of the path (single-result array unrolling): wrapped in `@()` and cast to string.

   - CLIXML stderr counted as a profile error: stripped in `Invoke-PSCode`.
   - toolkit.json broke on unescaped quotes in my own edit: fixed.

import re
p='99-verify.ps1'
s=open(p,encoding='ascii').read()
a=s.index("Test-Section 'E MCP launch commands (names only)' {")
b=s.index("# ------------------------------------------------------------------ lock")
new='''Test-Section 'E MCP launch commands (names only)' {
    $inv = Get-McpInventory
    $seen = 0
    foreach ($r in $inv) {
        $id = 'mcp-' + $r.file + '-' + $r.name
        if ($r.name -like '(*') { Add-Result $id 'INFO' $r.note; continue }
        $seen++
        if (-not $r.command) { Add-Result $id 'INFO' $r.note @{ purpose = $r.purpose }; continue }
        if ($r.resolves) { Add-Result $id 'PASS' ("command '{0}' resolves" -f $r.command) @{ command = $r.command; purpose = $r.purpose } }
        else { Add-Result $id 'WARN' ("command '{0}' does not resolve" -f $r.command) @{ command = $r.command } }
    }
    Add-Result 'mcp-total' 'INFO' ("{0} server entries inspected (names and command names only; args, env and urls are never read into output)" -f $seen)
}

'''
s=s[:a]+new+s[b:]
open(p,'w',encoding='ascii',newline='\n').write(s)
print('ok')
EOF
